Horizon-Evolution

Models: LCDM, PBUF

Horizon evolution prediction: physical and comoving Hubble radii, and a truncated comoving particle horizon as functions of redshift. These quantities are derived from the background expansion H(z) and the speed of light c, and illustrate the changing horizon scales in the chosen cosmological model.

Acceleration-Onset

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The acceleration-onset curve shows the redshift where cosmic deceleration transitions into acceleration. PBUF predicts this transition occurs earlier than in ΛCDM because elastic expansion begins contributing before dark-energy-like behavior would normally appear. This shift is a direct signature of spacetime stiffness replacing Λ. Observational confirmation would validate PBUF's elastic-driven acceleration.

Acceleration-Slowdown

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The slowdown curve measures when cosmic acceleration stops increasing and begins to flatten or decline. In PBUF this happens earlier because elastic saturation limits late-time acceleration. A measured slowdown consistent with the curve would strongly support PBUF's saturation physics and challenge ΛCDM's perpetual acceleration.

Age-Z

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The cosmic age curve t(z) shows how the universe ages alongside galaxy formation. It highlights the present-day age, key epochs like z=1 and z=6, and the redshift where the universe reaches half of its current age, connecting directly to observed galaxy ages and formation histories.

Ap-Distortion

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The Alcock–Paczynski distortion parameter F(z) tracks the relation between transverse and radial distances. It combines the angular diameter distance D_A(z) with H(z) and c to expose BAO anisotropy signatures that differ between PBUF and ΛCDM. The curve highlights geometry-only constraints from DESI, Euclid, and other surveys.

Bao-Shift

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The BAO-shift plot shows how the BAO distance scale differs between PBUF and ΛCDM. PBUF predicts a modest percent-level shift due to elastic-wave propagation delaying early-universe sound travel. This is directly testable by DESI and future BAO surveys. Matching the predicted offset would confirm PBUF's elastic modification of early-universe distances.

Cmb-Lensing

Models: LCDM, PBUF

CMB lensing prediction module. This module computes the convergence power spectrum C_ell^{kappa kappa} for the current cosmological model using the model's own lensing backend. It applies masking to remove non-physical or numerically unstable points and summarizes the total lensing strength via an effective amplitude A_L_eff. No external Planck defaults or hard-coded parameters are used.

Curvature

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The curvature evolution curve shows how PBUF predicts a small, persistent residual curvature generated by elastic expansion. This residual is not a free parameter but emerges naturally from the model. A measured curvature at the predicted level would support PBUF's curvature-elasticity link.

Curvature-Identity

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The curvature identity plot demonstrates the tight internal relationship between α, Ω_b0, and k_sat. Observational agreement confirms the internal consistency of the elastic medium.

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The dust-onset curve estimates the cumulative optical depth of intergalactic dust as a function of redshift. PBUF predicts earlier dust influence because of altered early-time temperature scaling. Upcoming JWST and ALMA observations can test whether dust buildup matches this predicted trend.

Dust-Temperature

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The dust-temperature prediction shows how dust grain temperature evolves under PBUF's modified radiation background. Because elastic cosmology alters early thermal history, dust temperatures at high redshift are expected to be higher than ΛCDM predicts. This is directly testable with far-infrared measurements.

Galaxy-Size

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The galaxy-size curve predicts that galaxies at high redshift should appear larger in PBUF than in ΛCDM due to modified angular-diameter distance and smoother early collapse. A systematic excess in observed galaxy sizes at z > 4 would strongly support PBUF's distance-redshift relation.

Growth

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The growth-factor prediction shows how linear structure growth evolves under PBUF. Elastic expansion suppresses growth slightly relative to ΛCDM, producing a shallower D(z) curve. This behavior is measurable through galaxy clustering and weak lensing and serves as a key test of elastic cosmology.

Growth-Index

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The growth-index curve γ(z) highlights the difference between elastic cosmology and ΛCDM in how structure grows. PBUF predicts a rising γ at z > 1 due to elastic suppression, unlike ΛCDM's nearly constant value. This upturn is a clean observational discriminator using DESI, Euclid, and SKA.

Gw-Propagation

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The GW propagation prediction shows that gravitational waves and electromagnetic waves arrive simultaneously in PBUF and share identical luminosity distances. This reflects spacetime's rigid wave medium. Any deviation from unity would challenge PBUF; agreement strengthens the model's wave-medium foundation.

High-Z-Delay

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The high-z delay curve shows how elastic-wave speed affects photon travel time from early epochs. PBUF predicts an accumulated delay relative to ΛCDM because c_eff was lower in the hotter early medium. Detection of systematic timing offsets at high redshift would support the elastic propagation model.

Isw-Cross

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The ISW-cross prediction shows how PBUF modifies the integrated Sachs-Wolfe signal and its cross-correlation with galaxy surveys. Elastic curvature produces a slightly different decay of gravitational potentials, yielding a measurable shift in the ISW kernel. Future CMB-LSS cross-correlation measurements can test this directly.

Metallicity

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The metallicity prediction shows that early-universe metallicity should be higher in PBUF due to accelerated star formation efficiency and delayed collapse times. This predicts more enriched galaxies at high redshift than ΛCDM allows. JWST measurements of early metallicity can directly confirm or falsify this.

Planck-Length

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The Planck-length evolution curve reflects how PBUF's temperature-dependent rigidity produces a mild, predictable variation in the effective Planck scale over cosmic time. This is a purely theoretical prediction but serves as an internal consistency test linking the quantum and cosmological sectors.

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The q-curve prediction tracks the deceleration parameter q(z) computed from the normalized expansion rate E(a). It highlights the onset of cosmic acceleration and exposes how PBUF diverges from ΛCDM across the sampled redshift range.

Redshift-Drift

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The redshift-drift prediction shows how the change in observed redshift over time differs slightly between PBUF and ΛCDM. Elasticity modifies H(z) subtly, producing a measurable drift signature for future 20-30 year surveys like ELT. Agreement with this curve would strongly support elastic expansion.

Sound-Horizon

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The sound-horizon prediction r_d shows how the early-universe sound horizon shrinks slightly in PBUF due to modified wave speed in the denser early medium. This shift naturally alleviates the H0 tension. Precision BAO analyses can measure whether r_d matches this predicted reduction.

Starburst-Efficiency

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The starburst-efficiency curve quantifies how elastic cosmology enhances high-redshift starburst activity. Earlier collapse and modified wave propagation increase the efficiency of star formation compared to ΛCDM. Observations of extreme star-formers at z > 4 can test this prediction.

Void-Size

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The void-size prediction shows that cosmic voids should be slightly larger in PBUF than in ΛCDM due to altered growth history and curvature. This percent-level difference is measurable with DESI and Euclid void catalogs and provides another geometric test of elastic cosmology.

Wave-Speed

Models: LCDM, PBUF

The wave-speed prediction shows how the effective propagation speed c_eff(z)/c evolves in PBUF's elastic medium. Light and gravitational waves traveled slower in the early universe due to lower rigidity. This produces cumulative timing effects and modifies early-universe horizons. Testing this requires precision distance and timing measurements.
